<div class="col-sm order-1"><h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 62Q. HEALTH PLAN COMPANIES</h2><div class="sr" id="stat.62Q.001"><b>62Q.001</b> MS 2006 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.001">15.001</a>]</div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.01"><h1 class="shn">62Q.01 DEFINITIONS.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2><p>For purposes of this chapter, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.1a"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Affordable Care Act.</span></h2><p>"Affordable Care Act" means the Affordable Care Act as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.011#stat.62A.011.1a">62A.011, subdivision 1a</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.1b"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Bona fide association.</span></h2><p>"Bona fide association" means an association that meets all of the following criteria:</p><p>(1) serves a single profession that requires a significant amount of education, training, or experience, or a license or certificate from a state authority to practice that profession;</p><p>(2) has been actively in existence for five years;</p><p>(3) has a constitution and bylaws or other analogous governing documents;</p><p>(4) has been formed and maintained in good faith for purposes other than obtaining insurance;</p><p>(5) is not owned or controlled by a health plan company or affiliated with a health plan company;</p><p>(6) does not condition membership in the association on any health status-related factor;</p><p>(7) has at least 1,000 members if it is a national association, 500 members if it is a state association, or 200 members if it is a local association;</p><p>(8) all members and dependents of members are eligible for coverage regardless of any health status-related factor;</p><p>(9) does not make health plans offered through the association available other than in connection with a member of the association;</p><p>(10) is governed by a board of directors and sponsors an annual meeting of its members; and</p><p>(11) produces only market association memberships, accepts applications for membership, or signs up members in the professional association where the subject individuals are actively engaged in, or directly related to, the profession represented by the association.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2><p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of health for purposes of regulating health maintenance organizations, and community integrated service networks, or the commissioner of commerce for purposes of regulating all other health plan companies. For all other purposes, "commissioner" means the commissioner of health.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.2a"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.</h2><p>MS 2012 [Renumbered subd 2b]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.2a"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Dependent child to the limiting age.</span></h2><p>"Dependent child to the limiting age" or "dependent children to the limiting age" means those individuals who are eligible and covered as a dependent child under the terms of a health plan who have not yet attained 26 years of age. A health plan company must not deny or restrict eligibility for a dependent child to the limiting age based on financial dependency, residency, marital status, or student status. For coverage under plans offered by the Minnesota Comprehensive Health Association, dependent to the limiting age means dependent as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.302#stat.62A.302.3">62A.302, subdivision 3</a>. Notwithstanding the provisions in this subdivision, a health plan may include:</p><p>(1) eligibility requirements regarding the absence of other health plan coverage as permitted by the Affordable Care Act for grandfathered plan coverage; or</p><p>(2) an age greater than 26 in its policy, contract, or certificate of coverage.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.2b"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Enrollee.</span></h2><p>"Enrollee" means a natural person covered by a health plan and includes an insured, policyholder, subscriber, contract holder, member, covered person, or certificate holder.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.2c"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2c.<span class="headnote">Grandfathered plan.</span></h2><p>"Grandfathered plan" means a health plan as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.011#stat.62A.011.1b">62A.011, subdivision 1b</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.2d"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2d.<span class="headnote">Group health plan.</span></h2><p>"Group health plan" means a group health plan as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.011#stat.62A.011.1c">62A.011, subdivision 1c</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Health plan.</span></h2><p>"Health plan" means a health plan as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.011">62A.011</a> or a policy, contract, or certificate issued by a community integrated service network.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.4"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Health plan company.</span></h2><p>"Health plan company" means:</p><p>(1) a health carrier as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.011#stat.62A.011.2">62A.011, subdivision 2</a>; or</p><p>(2) a community integrated service network as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62N.02#stat.62N.02.4a">62N.02, subdivision 4a</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.4a"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">High deductible health plans.</span></h2><p>"High deductible health plans" means those health coverage plans issued by a health plan company as defined under the provisions of sections 220 and 223 of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, and implementing regulations.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.4b"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4b.<span class="headnote">Individual health plan.</span></h2><p>"Individual health plan" means an individual health plan as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.011#stat.62A.011.4">62A.011, subdivision 4</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.4c"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4c.<span class="headnote">Life-threatening condition.</span></h2><p>"Life-threatening condition" means a disease or condition from which the likelihood of death is probable unless the course of the disease or condition is interrupted.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.5"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Managed care organization.</span></h2><p>"Managed care organization" means: (1) a health maintenance organization operating under chapter 62D; (2) a community integrated service network as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62N.02#stat.62N.02.4a">62N.02, subdivision 4a</a>; or (3) an insurance company licensed under chapter 60A, nonprofit health service plan corporation operating under chapter 62C, fraternal benefit society operating under chapter 64B, or any other health plan company, to the extent that it covers health care services delivered to Minnesota residents through a preferred provider organization or a network of selected providers.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.6"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Medicare-related coverage.</span></h2><p>"Medicare-related coverage" means a policy, contract, or certificate issued as a supplement to Medicare, regulated under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.3099">62A.3099</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.44">62A.44</a>, including Medicare select coverage; policies, contracts, or certificates that supplement Medicare issued by health maintenance organizations; or policies, contracts, or certificates governed by sections 1833 (known as "cost" or "HCPP" contracts), 1851 to 1859 (Medicare Advantage), 1860D (Medicare Part D), or 1876 (known as "TEFRA" or "risk" contracts) of the federal Social Security Act, United States Code, title 42, section 1395, et seq., as amended; or Section 4001 of the Balanced Budget Act of 1997 (BBA)(Public Law 105-33), Sections 1851 to 1859 of the Social Security Act establishing Part C of the Medicare program, known as the "Medicare Advantage program."</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.6a"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd 6a.<span class="headnote">Nonquantitative treatment limitations or NQTLs.</span></h2><p>"Nonquantitative treatment limitations" or "NQTLs" means processes, strategies, or evidentiary standards, or other factors that are not expressed numerically, but otherwise limit the scope or duration of benefits for treatment. NQTLs include but are not limited to:</p><p>(1) medical management standards limiting or excluding benefits based on (i) medical necessity or medical appropriateness, or (ii) whether the treatment is experimental or investigative;</p><p>(2) formulary design for prescription drugs;</p><p>(3) health plans with multiple network tiers;</p><p>(4) criteria and parameters for provider inclusion in provider networks, including credentialing standards and reimbursement rates;</p><p>(5) health plan methods for determining usual, customary, and reasonable charges;</p><p>(6) fail-first or step therapy protocols;</p><p>(7) exclusions based on failure to complete a course of treatment;</p><p>(8) restrictions based on geographic location, facility type, provider specialty, and other criteria that limit the scope or duration of benefits for services provided under the health plan;</p><p>(9) in-network and out-of-network geographic limitations;</p><p>(10) standards for providing access to out-of-network providers;</p><p>(11) limitations on inpatient services for situations where the enrollee is a threat to self or others;</p><p>(12) exclusions for court-ordered and involuntary holds;</p><p>(13) experimental treatment limitations;</p><p>(14) service coding;</p><p>(15) exclusions for services provided by clinical social workers; and</p><p>(16) provider reimbursement rates, including rates of reimbursement for mental health and substance use disorder services in primary care.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.6b"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6b.<span class="headnote">No Surprises Act.</span></h2><p>"No Surprises Act" means Division BB of the Consolidated Appropriations Act, 2021, which amended Title XXVII of the Public Health Service Act, Public Law 116-260, and any amendments to and any federal guidance or regulations issued under this act.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.01.7"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Primary care provider.</span></h2><p>"Primary care provider" means a health care professional who specializes in the practice of family medicine, general internal medicine, obstetrics and gynecology, or general pediatrics and is a licensed physician, a licensed and certified advanced practice registered nurse, or a licensed physician assistant.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.01.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/625/#laws.2.14.0">1994 c 625 art 2 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.2.2.0">1995 c 234 art 2 s 2</a>-6; art 3 s 5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/225/#laws.2.37.0">1997 c 225 art 2 s 37</a>-39,62; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/254/#laws.1.15.0">1998 c 254 art 1 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/215/#laws.0.26.0">2001 c 215 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/268/#laws.0.7.0">2004 c 268 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/17/#laws.1.14.0">2005 c 17 art 1 s 14</a>; art 3 s 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/84/#laws.1.60.0">2013 c 84 art 1 s 60</a>-67; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.8.12.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 8 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.2.23.0">2023 c 70 art 2 s 23</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.02"><h1 class="shn">62Q.02 APPLICABILITY OF CHAPTER.</h1><p>(a) This chapter applies only to health plans, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.01">62Q.01</a>, and not to other types of insurance issued or renewed by health plan companies, unless otherwise specified.</p><p>(b) This chapter applies to a health plan company only with respect to health plans, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.01">62Q.01</a>, issued or renewed by the health plan company, unless otherwise specified.</p><p>(c) If a health plan company issues or renews health plans in other states, this chapter applies only to health plans issued or renewed in this state for Minnesota residents, or to cover a resident of the state, unless otherwise specified.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.02.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.2.7.0">1995 c 234 art 2 s 7</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.021"><h1 class="shn">62Q.021 FEDERAL ACT; COMPLIANCE REQUIRED.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.021.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Compliance with 1996 federal law.</span></h2><p>Each health plan company shall comply with the federal Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act of 1996, including any federal regulations adopted under that act, to the extent that it imposes a requirement that applies in this state and that is not also required by the laws of this state. This section does not require compliance with any provision of the federal act prior to the effective date provided for that provision in the federal act. The commissioner shall enforce this subdivision.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.021.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Compliance with 2010 federal law.</span></h2><p>Each health plan company shall comply with the Affordable Care Act to the extent that it imposes a requirement that applies in this state but is not required under the laws of this state. This section does not require compliance with any provision of the Affordable Care Act before the effective date provided for that provision in the Affordable Care Act. The commissioner shall enforce this subdivision.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.021.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Compliance with 2021 federal law.</span></h2><p>Each health plan company, health provider, and health facility shall comply with the No Surprises Act, including any federal regulations adopted under the act, to the extent that the act imposes requirements that apply in this state but are not required under the laws of this state. This subdivision does not require compliance with any provision of the No Surprises Act before the effective date provided for that provision in the No Surprises Act. The commissioner shall enforce this subdivision.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.021.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/175/#laws.4.2.0">1997 c 175 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/84/#laws.1.68.0">2013 c 84 art 1 s 68</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.2.24.0">2023 c 70 art 2 s 24</a></p></div><h1 class="fgh">HIGH DEDUCTIBLE HEALTH PLANS</h1><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.025"><h1 class="shn">62Q.025 HIGH DEDUCTIBLE HEALTH PLANS.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.025.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Qualified plan.</span></h2><p>A high deductible health plan shall be deemed a qualified plan under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62E.06">62E.06</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62E.12">62E.12</a>. The plan must meet all other requirements of state law except those that are inconsistent with a high deductible health plan as defined in sections 220 and 223 of the Internal Revenue Code and supporting regulations.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.025.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Authorization.</span></h2><p>Notwithstanding any other law of this state, any health plan company defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.01#stat.62Q.01.4">62Q.01, subdivision 4</a>, is permitted to offer high deductible health plans.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.025.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/268/#laws.0.8.0">2004 c 268 s 8</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.03"><h1 class="shn">62Q.03 PROCESS FOR RISK ADJUSTMENT SYSTEM.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2><p>The purpose of risk adjustment is to reduce the effects of risk selection on health insurance premiums by making monetary transfers from health plan companies that insure lower risk populations to health plan companies that insure higher risk populations. Risk adjustment is needed to: achieve a more equitable, efficient system of health care financing; remove current disincentives in the health care system to insure and provide adequate access for high risk and special needs populations; promote fair competition among health plan companies on the basis of their ability to efficiently and effectively provide services rather than on the risk status of those in a given insurance pool; and help maintain the viability of health plan companies, by protecting them from the financial effects of enrolling a disproportionate number of high risk individuals. It is the commitment of the state to develop and implement a risk adjustment system. The risk adjustment system shall:</p><p>(1) possess a reasonable level of accuracy and administrative feasibility, be adaptable to changes as methods improve, incorporate safeguards against fraud and manipulation, and shall neither reward inefficiency nor penalize for verifiable improvements in health status;</p><p>(2) require participation by all health plan companies providing coverage in the individual, small group, and Medicare supplement markets;</p><p>(3) address unequal distribution of risk between health plan companies, but shall not address the financing of public programs or subsidies for low-income people; and</p><p>(4) be developed and implemented by the Risk Adjustment Association with joint oversight by the commissioners of health and commerce.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2><p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.2.36.0">1995 c 234 art 2 s 36</a>]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2><p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.2.36.0">1995 c 234 art 2 s 36</a>]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.4"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2><p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.2.36.0">1995 c 234 art 2 s 36</a>]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.5"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2><p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.2.36.0">1995 c 234 art 2 s 36</a>]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.5a"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Public programs.</span></h2><p>(a) A separate risk adjustment system must be developed for state-run public programs, including medical assistance and MinnesotaCare. The system must be developed in accordance with the general risk adjustment methodologies described in this section, must include factors in addition to age and sex adjustment, and may include additional demographic factors, different targeted conditions, and/or different payment amounts for conditions. The risk adjustment system for public programs must attempt to reflect the special needs related to poverty, cultural, or language barriers and other needs of the public program population.</p><p>(b) The commissioner of human services shall phase in risk adjustment according to the following schedule:</p><p>(1) for the first contract year, no more than ten percent of reimbursements shall be risk adjusted; and</p><p>(2) for the second contract year, no more than 30 percent of reimbursements shall be risk adjusted.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.5b"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5b.<span class="headnote">Medicare supplement market.</span></h2><p>A risk adjustment system may be developed for the Medicare supplement market. The Medicare supplement risk adjustment system may include a demographic component and may, but is not required to, include a condition-specific risk adjustment component.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.6"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Creation of Risk Adjustment Association.</span></h2><p>The Minnesota Risk Adjustment Association is created on July 1, 1994, and may operate as a nonprofit unincorporated association, but is authorized to incorporate under chapter 317A.</p><p>The provisions of this chapter govern if the provisions of chapter 317A conflict with this chapter. The association may operate under the approved plan of operation and shall be governed in accordance with this chapter and may operate in accordance with chapter 317A. If the association incorporates as a nonprofit corporation under chapter 317A, the filing of the plan of operation meets the requirements of filing articles of incorporation.</p><p>The association, its transactions, and all property owned by it are exempt from taxation under the laws of this state or any of its subdivisions, including, but not limited to, income tax, sales tax, use tax, and property tax. The association may seek exemption from payment of all fees and taxes levied by the federal government. Except as otherwise provided in this chapter, the association is not subject to the provisions of chapters 14, 60A, and 62A. The association is not a public employer and is not subject to the provisions of chapters 179A and 353. The board of directors and health carriers who are members of the association are exempt from sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/325D.49">325D.49</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/325D.66">325D.66</a> in the performance of their duties as directors and members of the association. The Risk Adjustment Association is subject to the Open Meeting Law.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.7"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Purpose of association.</span></h2><p>The association is established to develop and implement a private sector risk adjustment system.</p><p>Subject to state oversight set forth in subdivision 10, the association shall:</p><p>(1) develop and implement comprehensive risk adjustment systems for individual, small group, and Medicare supplement markets consistent with the provisions of this chapter;</p><p>(2) submit a plan for the development of the risk adjustment system which identifies appropriate implementation dates consistent with the rating and underwriting restrictions of each market, recommends whether transfers attributable to risk adjustment should be required between the individual and small group markets, and makes other appropriate recommendations to the commissioners of health and commerce by November 5, 1995;</p><p>(3) develop a combination of a demographic risk adjustment system and payments for targeted conditions;</p><p>(4) test an ambulatory care groups (ACGs) and diagnostic cost groups (DCGs) system, and recommend whether such a methodology should be adopted;</p><p>(5) fund the development and testing of the risk adjustment system;</p><p>(6) recommend market conduct guidelines; and</p><p>(7) develop a plan for assessing members for the costs of administering the risk adjustment system.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.8"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Governance.</span></h2><p>The association shall be governed according to the plan of operation as established in subdivision 8a.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.8a"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8a.<span class="headnote">Plan of operation.</span></h2><p>The board shall submit a proposed plan of operation by August 15, 1995, to the commissioners of health and commerce for review. The commissioners of health and commerce shall have the authority to approve or reject the plan of operation.</p><p>Amendments to the plan of operation may be made by the commissioners or by the directors of the association, subject to the approval of the commissioners.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.9"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Data collection and data privacy.</span></h2><p>The association members shall not have access to unaggregated data on individuals or health plan companies. The association shall develop, as a part of the plan of operation, procedures for ensuring that data is collected by an appropriate entity. The commissioners of health and commerce shall have the authority to audit and examine data collected by the association for the purposes of the development and implementation of the risk adjustment system. Data on individuals obtained for the purposes of risk adjustment development, testing, and operation are designated as private data. Data not on individuals which is obtained for the purposes of development, testing, and operation of risk adjustment are designated as nonpublic data, except that the proposed and approved plan of operation, the risk adjustment methodologies examined, the plan for testing, the plan of the risk adjustment system, minutes of meetings, and other general operating information are classified as public data. Nothing in this section is intended to prohibit the preparation of summary data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.05#stat.13.05.7">13.05, subdivision 7</a>. The association, state agencies, and any contractors having access to this data shall maintain it in accordance with this classification. The commissioners of health and human services have the authority to collect data from health plan companies as needed for the purpose of developing a risk adjustment mechanism for public programs.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.10"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">State oversight of risk adjustment activities.</span></h2><p>The association's activities shall be supervised by the commissioners of health and commerce. The commissioners shall provide specific oversight functions during the development and implementation phases of the risk adjustment system as follows:</p><p>(1) the commissioners shall approve or reject the association's plan for testing risk adjustment methods, the methods to be used, and any changes to those methods;</p><p>(2) the commissioners must have the right to attend and participate in all meetings of the association and its work groups or committees, except for meetings involving privileged communication between the association and its counsel as permitted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13D.05#stat.13D.05.3">13D.05, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (b);</p><p>(3) the commissioners shall approve any consultants or administrators used by the association;</p><p>(4) the commissioners shall approve or reject the association's plan of operation; and</p><p>(5) the commissioners shall approve or reject the plan for the risk adjustment system described in subdivision 7, clause (2).</p><p>If the commissioners reject any of the plans identified in clauses (1), (4), and (5), the directors shall submit for review an appropriate revised plan within 30 days.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.11"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.</h2><p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.2.36.0">1995 c 234 art 2 s 36</a>]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.03.12"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Participation by all health plan companies.</span></h2><p>Upon its implementation, all health plan companies, as a condition of licensure, must participate in the risk adjustment system to be implemented under this section.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.03.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/625/#laws.2.15.0">1994 c 625 art 2 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.2.8.0">1995 c 234 art 2 s 8</a>-17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/440/#laws.1.33.0">1996 c 440 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/451/#laws.4.2.0">1996 c 451 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/192/#laws.0.18.0">1997 c 192 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/225/#laws.2.40.0">1997 c 225 art 2 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/254/#laws.1.16.0">1998 c 254 art 1 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/245/#laws.2.12.0">1999 c 245 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.15.0">2001 c 161 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.2.23.0">2016 c 158 art 2 s 23</a></p></div><div class="sr" id="stat.62Q.07"><b>62Q.07</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/170/#laws.0.11.0">2001 c 170 s 11</a>]</div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.075"><h1 class="shn">62Q.075 LOCAL PUBLIC ACCOUNTABILITY AND COLLABORATION PLAN.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.075.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2><p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/171/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 171 s 1</a>]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.075.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Requirement.</span></h2><p>Beginning October 31, 2004, all health maintenance organizations shall file a plan every four years with the commissioner of health describing the actions the health maintenance organization intends to take to contribute to achieving one or more high priority public health goals. This plan must be jointly developed in collaboration with the local public health units, and other community organizations providing health services within the same service area as the health maintenance organization. Local government units with responsibilities and authority defined under chapter 145A may designate individuals to participate in the collaborative planning with the health maintenance organization to provide expertise and represent community needs and goals as identified under chapter 145A. Every other year, beginning October 31, 2002, all health maintenance organizations shall file reports updating progress on the four-year collaboration plan.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.075.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Contents.</span></h2><p>The plan must address the following:</p><p>(1) specific measurement strategies and a description of any activities which contribute to one or more high priority public health goals;</p><p>(2) description of the process by which the health maintenance organization will coordinate its activities with the community health boards, and other relevant community organizations servicing the same area;</p><p>(3) documentation indicating that local public health units and local government unit designees were involved in the development of the plan; and</p><p>(4) documentation of compliance with the plan filed previously, including data on the previously identified progress measures.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.075.4"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Review.</span></h2><p>Upon receipt of the plan, the commissioner of health shall provide a copy to the local community health boards, and other relevant community organizations within the health maintenance organization's service area. After reviewing the plan, these community groups may submit written comments on the plan to the commissioner of health and may advise the commissioner of the health maintenance organization's effectiveness in assisting to achieve high priority public health goals. The plan may be reviewed by the county boards, or city councils acting as a community health board in accordance with chapter 145A, within the health maintenance organization's service area to determine whether the plan is consistent with the goals and objectives of the plans required under chapter 145A and whether the plan meets the needs of the community. The county board, or applicable city council, may also review and make recommendations on the availability and accessibility of services provided by the health maintenance organization. The county board, or applicable city council, may submit written comments to the commissioner of health, and may advise the commissioner of the health maintenance organization's effectiveness in assisting to meet the needs and goals as defined under the responsibilities of chapter 145A. Copies of these written comments must be provided to the health maintenance organization. The plan and any comments submitted must be filed with the information clearinghouse to be distributed to the public.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.075.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/625/#laws.7.1.0">1994 c 625 art 7 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.8.17.0">1995 c 234 art 8 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/451/#laws.4.3.0">1996 c 451 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/225/#laws.2.62.0">1997 c 225 art 2 s 62</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/245/#laws.2.13.0">1999 c 245 art 2 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/171/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 171 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/98/#laws.3.24.0">2005 c 98 art 3 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/21/#laws.1.109.0">2015 c 21 art 1 s 109</a></p></div><div class="sr" id="stat.62Q.09"><b>62Q.09</b> [Expired]</div><div class="sr" id="stat.62Q.095"><b>62Q.095</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/77/#laws.0.8.0">2005 c 77 s 8</a>]</div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.096"><h1 class="shn">62Q.096 CREDENTIALING OF PROVIDERS.</h1><p>(a) If a health plan company has initially credentialed, as providers in its provider network, individual providers employed by or under contract with an entity that:</p><p>(1) is authorized to bill under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625#stat.256B.0625.5">256B.0625, subdivision 5</a>;</p><p>(2) is a mental health clinic certified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.20">245I.20</a>;</p><p>(3) is designated an essential community provider under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.19">62Q.19</a>; and</p><p>(4) is under contract with the health plan company to provide mental health services, the health plan company must continue to credential at least the same number of providers from that entity, as long as those providers meet the health plan company's credentialing standards.</p><p>(b) In order to ensure timely access by patients to mental health services, between July 1, 2023, and June 30, 2025, a health plan company must credential and enter into a contract for mental health services with any provider of mental health services that:</p><p>(1) meets the health plan company's credential requirements. For purposes of credentialing under this paragraph, a health plan company may waive credentialing requirements that are not directly related to quality of care in order to ensure patient access to providers from underserved communities or to providers in rural areas;</p><p>(2) seeks to receive a credential from the health plan company;</p><p>(3) agrees to the health plan company's contract terms. The contract shall include payment rates that are usual and customary for the services provided;</p><p>(4) is accepting new patients; and</p><p>(5) is not already under a contract with the health plan company under a separate tax identification number or, if already under a contract with the health plan company, has provided notice to the health plan company of termination of the existing contract.</p><p class="in">(c) A health plan company shall not refuse to credential these providers on the grounds that their provider network has:</p><p class="in">(1) a sufficient number of providers of that type, including but not limited to the provider types identified in paragraph (a); or</p><p class="in">(2) a sufficient number of providers of mental health services in the aggregate.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.096.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/407/#laws.8.4.0">1998 c 407 art 8 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.17.3.0">2021 c 30 art 17 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/57/#laws.2.40.0">2023 c 57 art 2 s 40</a></p></div><p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to this section by Laws 2021, chapter 30, article 17, section 3, is effective upon federal approval. The commissioner of human services shall notify the revisor of statutes when federal approval is obtained. Laws 2021, chapter 30, article 17, section 114.</p><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.097"><h1 class="shn">62Q.097 REQUIREMENTS FOR TIMELY PROVIDER CREDENTIALING.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.097.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2><p>(a) The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p><p>(b) "Clean application for provider credentialing" or "clean application" means an application for provider credentialing submitted by a health care provider to a health plan company that is complete, is in the format required by the health plan company, and includes all information and substantiation required by the health plan company and does not require evaluation of any identified potential quality or safety concern.</p><p>(c) "Provider credentialing" means the process undertaken by a health plan company to evaluate and approve a health care provider's education, training, residency, licenses, certifications, and history of significant quality or safety concerns in order to approve the health care provider to provide health care services to patients at a clinic or facility.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.097.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Time limit for credentialing determination.</span></h2><p>A health plan company that receives an application for provider credentialing must:</p><p>(1) if the application is determined to be a clean application for provider credentialing and if the health care provider submitting the application or the clinic or facility at which the health care provider provides services requests the information, affirm that the health care provider's application is a clean application and notify the health care provider or clinic or facility of the date by which the health plan company will make a determination on the health care provider's application;</p><p>(2) if the application is determined not to be a clean application, inform the health care provider of the application's deficiencies or missing information or substantiation within three business days after the health plan company determines the application is not a clean application; and</p><p>(3) make a determination on the health care provider's clean application within 45 days after receiving the clean application unless the health plan company identifies a substantive quality or safety concern in the course of provider credentialing that requires further investigation. Upon notice to the health care provider, clinic, or facility, the health plan company is allowed 30 additional days to investigate any quality or safety concerns.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.097.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Prohibited application questions.</span></h2><p>An application for provider credentialing must not:</p><p>(1) require the provider to disclose past health conditions;</p><p>(2) require the provider to disclose current health conditions, if the provider is being treated so that the condition does not affect the provider's ability to practice medicine; or</p><p>(3) require the disclosure of any health conditions that would not affect the provider's ability to practice medicine in a competent, safe, and ethical manner.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.097.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.6.1.0">2021 c 30 art 6 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.57.32.0">2024 c 127 art 57 s 32</a></p></div><div class="sr" id="stat.62Q.10"><b>62Q.10</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/187/#laws.1.75.0">2012 c 187 art 1 s 75</a>]</div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.101"><h1 class="shn">62Q.101 EVALUATION OF PROVIDER PERFORMANCE.</h1><p class="in">A health plan company, or a vendor of risk management services as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/60A.23#stat.60A.23.8">60A.23, subdivision 8</a>, shall, in evaluating the performance of a health care provider:</p><p class="in">(1) conduct the evaluation using a bona fide baseline based upon practice experience of the provider group; and</p><p class="in">(2) disclose the baseline to the health care provider in writing and prior to the beginning of the time period used for the evaluation.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.101.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.15.10.0">2007 c 147 art 15 s 10</a></p></div><div class="sr" id="stat.62Q.105"><b>62Q.105</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/239/#laws.0.43.0">1999 c 239 s 43</a>]</div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.1055"><h1 class="shn">62Q.1055 CHEMICAL DEPENDENCY.</h1><p>All health plan companies shall use the assessment criteria in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245G.05">245G.05</a> when assessing and treating enrollees for chemical dependency treatment.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.1055.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.2.22.0">1995 c 234 art 2 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.214.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 214</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/50/#laws.2.2.0">2023 c 50 art 2 s 2</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.106"><h1 class="shn">62Q.106 DISPUTE RESOLUTION BY COMMISSIONER.</h1><p>(a) A complainant may at any time submit a complaint to the appropriate commissioner to investigate. After investigating a complaint, or reviewing a company's decision, the appropriate commissioner may order a remedy as authorized under chapter 45, 60A, or 62D.</p><p>(b) In investigating a complaint filed against a health maintenance organization regarding a vulnerable adult, upon request, the commissioner of health must interview at least one family member of the complainant or the subject of the complaint. If the complainant or the subject of the complaint does not want any family members to be interviewed, this information will be included in the investigative file.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.106.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.2.23.0">1995 c 234 art 2 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/225/#laws.2.41.0">1997 c 225 art 2 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/239/#laws.0.32.0">1999 c 239 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/43/#laws.0.2.0">2013 c 43 s 2</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.107"><h1 class="shn">62Q.107 PROHIBITED PROVISION; JUDICIAL REVIEW.</h1><p>Beginning January 1, 1999, no health plan, including the coverages described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.011#stat.62A.011.3">62A.011, subdivision 3</a>, clauses (7) and (10), may specify a standard of review upon which a court may review denial of a claim or of any other decision made by a health plan company with respect to an enrollee. This section prohibits limiting court review to a determination of whether the health plan company's decision is arbitrary and capricious, an abuse of discretion, or any other standard less favorable to the enrollee than a preponderance of the evidence.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.107.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/407/#laws.2.21.0">1998 c 407 art 2 s 21</a></p></div><div class="sr" id="stat.62Q.11"><b>62Q.11</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/239/#laws.0.43.0">1999 c 239 s 43</a>]</div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.12"><h1 class="shn">62Q.12 DENIAL OF ACCESS.</h1><p>No health plan company may deny access to a covered health care service unless the denial is made by, or under the direction of, or subject to the review of a health care professional licensed to provide the service in question.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.12.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/625/#laws.2.20.0">1994 c 625 art 2 s 20</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.121"><h1 class="shn">62Q.121 LICENSURE OF MEDICAL DIRECTORS.</h1><p>(a) No health plan company may employ a person as a medical director unless the person is licensed as a physician in this state. This section does not apply to a health plan company that is assessed less than three percent of the total amount assessed by the Minnesota Comprehensive Health Association.</p><p>(b) For purposes of this section, "medical director" means a physician employed by a health plan company who has direct decision-making authority, based upon medical training and knowledge, regarding the health plan company's medical protocols, medical policies, or coverage of treatment of a particular enrollee, regardless of the physician's title.</p><p>(c) This section applies only to medical directors who make recommendations or decisions that involve or affect enrollees who live in this state.</p><p>(d) Each health plan company that is subject to this section shall provide the commissioner with the names and licensure information of its medical directors and shall provide updates no later than 30 days after any changes.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.121.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.16.6.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 16 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.113.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 113</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.135"><h1 class="shn">62Q.135 CONTRACTING FOR CHEMICAL DEPENDENCY SERVICES.</h1><p>No health plan company shall contract with a chemical dependency treatment program, unless the program participates in the chemical dependency treatment accountability plan established by the commissioner of human services. The commissioner of human services shall make data on chemical dependency services and outcomes collected through this program available to health plan companies.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.135.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/625/#laws.2.21.0">1994 c 625 art 2 s 21</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.137"><h1 class="shn">62Q.137 CHEMICAL DEPENDENCY TREATMENT; COVERAGE.</h1><p>(a) Any health plan that provides coverage for chemical dependency treatment must cover chemical dependency treatment provided to an enrollee by the Department of Corrections while the enrollee is committed to the custody of the commissioner of corrections following a conviction for a first-degree driving while impaired offense under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.24">169A.24</a> if: (1) a court of competent jurisdiction makes a preliminary determination based on a chemical use assessment conducted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169A.70">169A.70</a> that treatment may be appropriate and includes this determination as part of the sentencing order; and (2) the Department of Corrections makes a determination based on a chemical assessment conducted while the individual is in the custody of the department that treatment is appropriate. Treatment provided by the Department of Corrections that meets the requirements of this section shall not be subject to a separate medical necessity determination under the health plan company's utilization review procedures.</p><p>(b) The health plan company must be given a copy of the court's preliminary determination and supporting documents and the assessment conducted by the Department of Corrections.</p><p>(c) Payment rates for treatment provided by the Department of Corrections shall not exceed the lowest rate for outpatient chemical dependency treatment paid by the health plan company to a participating provider of the health plan company.</p><p>(d) For purposes of this section, chemical dependency treatment means all covered services that are intended to treat chemical dependency and that are covered by the enrollee's health plan or by law.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.137.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.19.1.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 19 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.113.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 113</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.14"><h1 class="shn">62Q.14 RESTRICTIONS ON ENROLLEE SERVICES.</h1><p>No health plan company may restrict the choice of an enrollee as to where the enrollee receives services related to:</p><p>(1) the voluntary planning of the conception and bearing of children;</p><p>(2) the diagnosis of infertility;</p><p>(3) the testing and treatment of a sexually transmitted disease; and</p><p>(4) the testing for AIDS or other HIV-related conditions.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.14.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/625/#laws.2.22.0">1994 c 625 art 2 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.57.33.0">2024 c 127 art 57 s 33</a></p></div><div class="sr" id="stat.62Q.145"><b>62Q.145</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.16"><h1 class="shn">62Q.16 MIDMONTH TERMINATION PROHIBITED.</h1><p>The termination of a person's coverage under any health plan as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.011#stat.62A.011.3">62A.011, subdivision 3</a>, with the exception of individual health plans, issued or renewed on or after January 1, 1995, must provide coverage until the end of the month in which coverage was terminated.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.16.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/625/#laws.2.23.0">1994 c 625 art 2 s 23</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.165"><h1 class="shn">62Q.165 UNIVERSAL COVERAGE.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.165.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2><p class="in">It is the commitment of the state to achieve universal health coverage for all Minnesotans by the year 2011. Universal coverage is achieved when:</p><p class="in">(1) every Minnesotan has access to a full range of quality health care services;</p><p class="in">(2) every Minnesotan is able to obtain affordable health coverage which pays for the full range of services, including preventive and primary care; and</p><p class="in">(3) every Minnesotan pays into the health care system according to that person's ability.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.165.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Goal.</span></h2><p class="in">It is the goal of the state to make continuous progress toward reducing the number of Minnesotans who do not have health coverage so that by January 1, 2011, all Minnesota residents have access to affordable health care. The goal will be achieved by improving access to private health coverage through insurance reforms and market reforms, by making health coverage more affordable for low-income Minnesotans through purchasing pools and state subsidies, and by reducing the cost of health coverage through cost containment programs and methods of ensuring that all Minnesotans are paying into the system according to their ability.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.165.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2><p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/225/#laws.2.63.0">1997 c 225 art 2 s 63</a>]</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.165.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/625/#laws.6.1.0">1994 c 625 art 6 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.4.1.0">1995 c 234 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.15.11.0">2007 c 147 art 15 s 11</a>,12</p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.17"><h1 class="shn">62Q.17 VOLUNTARY PURCHASING POOLS.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.17.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Permission to form.</span></h2><p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.10">62A.10</a>, employers, groups, and individuals may voluntarily form purchasing pools, solely for the purpose of negotiating and purchasing health plan coverage from health plan companies for members of the pool.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.17.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Common factors.</span></h2><p>All participants in a purchasing pool must live within a common geographic region, be employed in a similar occupation, or share some other common factor as approved by the commissioner of commerce. The membership criteria must not be designed to include disproportionately employers, groups, or individuals likely to have low costs of health coverage, or to exclude disproportionately employers, groups, or individuals likely to have high costs of health coverage.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.17.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Governing structure.</span></h2><p>Each pool must have a governing structure controlled by its members. The governing structure of the pool is responsible for administration of the pool. The governing structure shall review and evaluate all bids for coverage from health plan companies, shall determine criteria for joining and leaving the pool, and may design incentives for healthy lifestyles and health promotion programs. The governing structure may design uniform entrance standards for all employers, except small employers as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62L.02">62L.02</a>. Small employers must be permitted to enter any pool if the small employer meets the pool's membership requirements. Pools must provide as much choice in health plans to members as is financially possible. The governing structure may charge all members a fee for administrative purposes.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.17.4"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Enrollment.</span></h2><p>Pools must have an annual open enrollment period of not less than 15 days, during which all individuals or groups that qualify for membership may enter the pool without any preexisting condition limitations or exclusions or exclusionary riders, except those permitted under chapter 62L for groups or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.65">62A.65</a> for individuals. Pools must reach and maintain an enrolled population of at least 1,000 members within six months of formation. If a pool fails to reach or maintain the minimum enrollment, all coverage subsequently purchased through the purchasing pool must be regulated through existing applicable laws and forgo all advantages under this section.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.17.5"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Members.</span></h2><p>The governing structure of the pool shall set a minimum time period for membership. Members must stay in the purchasing pool for the entire minimum period to avoid paying a penalty. Penalties for early withdrawal from the purchasing pool shall be established by the governing structure.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.17.6"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Employer-based purchasing pools.</span></h2><p>Employer-based purchasing pools must, with respect to small employers as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62L.02">62L.02</a>, meet all the requirements of chapter 62L. The experience of the pool must be pooled and the rates blended across all groups.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.17.7"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Individual members.</span></h2><p>Purchasing pools that contain individual members must meet all of the underwriting and rate restrictions found in the individual health plan market.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.17.8"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Reports.</span></h2><p>Prior to the initial effective date of coverage, and annually on July 1 thereafter, each pool shall file a report with the information clearinghouse and the commissioner of commerce. The information clearinghouse must use the report to promote the purchasing pools. The annual report must contain the following information:</p><p>(1) the number of lives in the pool;</p><p>(2) the geographic area the pool intends to cover;</p><p>(3) the number of health plans offered;</p><p>(4) a description of the benefits under each plan;</p><p>(5) a description of the premium structure, including any co-payments or deductibles, of each plan offered;</p><p>(6) evidence of compliance with chapter 62L;</p><p>(7) a sample of marketing information, including a phone number where the pool may be contacted; and</p><p>(8) a list of all administrative fees charged.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.17.9"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2><p>Purchasing pools must register prior to offering coverage, and annually on July 1 thereafter, with the commissioner of commerce on a form prescribed by the commissioner. The commissioner of commerce shall enforce this section and all other state laws with respect to purchasing pools, and has for that purpose all general rulemaking and enforcement powers otherwise available to the commissioner of commerce. The commissioner may charge an annual registration fee sufficient to meet the costs of the commissioner's duties under this section.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.17.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/625/#laws.6.2.0">1994 c 625 art 6 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.7.24.0">1995 c 234 art 7 s 24</a>-26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/84/#laws.1.69.0">2013 c 84 art 1 s 69</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.18"><h1 class="shn">62Q.18 PORTABILITY OF COVERAGE.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.18.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2><p>For purposes of this section:</p><p>(1) "continuous coverage" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62L.02#stat.62L.02.9">62L.02, subdivision 9</a>;</p><p>(2) "guaranteed issue" means:</p><p>(i) for individual health plans, that a health plan company shall not decline an application by an individual for any individual health plan offered by that health plan company, including coverage for a dependent of the individual to whom the health plan has been or would be issued; and</p><p>(ii) for group health plans, that a health plan company shall not decline an application by a group for any group health plan offered by that health plan company and shall not decline to cover under the group health plan any person eligible for coverage under the group's eligibility requirements, including persons who become eligible after initial issuance of the group health plan; and</p><p>(3) "large employer" means an entity that would be a small employer, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62L.02#stat.62L.02.26">62L.02, subdivision 26</a>, except that the entity has more than 50 current employees, based upon the method provided in that subdivision for determining the number of current employees.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.18.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2><p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.4.4.0">1995 c 234 art 4 s 4</a>]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.18.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2><p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.4.4.0">1995 c 234 art 4 s 4</a>]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.18.4"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2><p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.4.4.0">1995 c 234 art 4 s 4</a>]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.18.5"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2><p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.4.4.0">1995 c 234 art 4 s 4</a>]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.18.6"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2><p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.4.4.0">1995 c 234 art 4 s 4</a>]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.18.7"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Portability of coverage.</span></h2><p>Effective July 1, 1994, no health plan company shall offer, sell, issue, or renew any group health plan that does not, with respect to individuals who maintain continuous coverage and who qualify under the group's eligibility requirements:</p><p>(1) make coverage available on a guaranteed issue basis;</p><p>(2) give full credit for previous continuous coverage against any applicable preexisting condition limitation or preexisting condition exclusion; and</p><p>(3) with respect to a group health plan offered, sold, issued, or renewed to a large employer, impose preexisting condition limitations or preexisting condition exclusions except to the extent that would be permitted under chapter 62L if the group sponsor were a small employer as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62L.02#stat.62L.02.26">62L.02, subdivision 26</a>.</p><p>To the extent that this subdivision conflicts with chapter 62L, chapter 62L governs, regardless of whether the group sponsor is a small employer as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62L.02">62L.02</a>, except that for group health plans issued to groups that are not small employers, this subdivision's requirement that the individual have maintained continuous coverage applies. An individual who has maintained continuous coverage, but would be considered a late entrant under chapter 62L, may be treated as a late entrant in the same manner under this subdivision as permitted under chapter 62L.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.18.8"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2><p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.4.4.0">1995 c 234 art 4 s 4</a>]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.18.9"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2><p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.4.4.0">1995 c 234 art 4 s 4</a>]</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.18.10"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Guaranteed issue.</span></h2><p>No health plan company shall offer, sell, or issue any health plan that does not make coverage available on a guaranteed issue basis in accordance with the Affordable Care Act.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.18.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/625/#laws.6.3.0">1994 c 625 art 6 s 3</a>; art 8 s 72; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/96/#laws.0.2.0">1995 c 96 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/234/#laws.4.2.0">1995 c 234 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/175/#laws.3.1.0">1997 c 175 art 3 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/225/#laws.2.63.0">1997 c 225 art 2 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/84/#laws.1.70.0">2013 c 84 art 1 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.21.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 21</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.181"><h1 class="shn">62Q.181 WRITTEN CERTIFICATION OF COVERAGE.</h1><p>A health plan company shall provide the written certifications of coverage required under United States Code, title 42, sections 300gg(e) and 300gg-43. This section applies only to coverage that is subject to regulation under state law and only to the extent that the certification of coverage is required under federal law. The commissioner shall enforce this section.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.181.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/175/#laws.4.3.0">1997 c 175 art 4 s 3</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.184"><h1 class="shn">62Q.184 STEP THERAPY OVERRIDE.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.184.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2><p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the terms in this subdivision have the meanings given them.</p><p>(b) "Clinical practice guideline" means a systematically developed statement to assist health care providers and enrollees in making decisions about appropriate health care services for specific clinical circumstances and conditions developed independently of a health plan company, pharmaceutical manufacturer, or any entity with a conflict of interest. A clinical practice guideline also includes a preferred drug list developed in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0625">256B.0625</a>.</p><p>(c) "Clinical review criteria" means the written screening procedures, decision abstracts, clinical protocols, and clinical practice guidelines used by a health plan company to determine the medical necessity and appropriateness of health care services.</p><p>(d) "Health plan company" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.01#stat.62Q.01.4">62Q.01, subdivision 4</a>, but also includes a county-based purchasing plan participating in a public program under chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B">256B</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256L">256L </a>and an integrated health partnership under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0755">256B.0755</a>.</p><p>(e) "Step therapy protocol" means a protocol or program that establishes the specific sequence in which prescription drugs for a specified medical condition, including self-administered drugs and drugs that are administered by a physician, advanced practice registered nurse, or physician assistant, are medically appropriate for a particular enrollee and are covered under a health plan.</p><p>(f) "Step therapy override" means that the step therapy protocol is overridden in favor of coverage of the selected prescription drug of the prescribing health care provider because at least one of the conditions of subdivision 3, paragraph (a), exists.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.184.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Establishment of a step therapy protocol.</span></h2><p>A health plan company shall consider available recognized evidence-based and peer-reviewed clinical practice guidelines when establishing a step therapy protocol. Upon written request of an enrollee, a health plan company shall provide any clinical review criteria applicable to a specific prescription drug covered by the health plan.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.184.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Step therapy override process; transparency.</span></h2><p>(a) When coverage of a prescription drug for the treatment of a medical condition is restricted for use by a health plan company through the use of a step therapy protocol, enrollees and prescribing health care providers shall have access to a clear, readily accessible, and convenient process to request a step therapy override. The process shall be made easily accessible on the health plan company's website. A health plan company may use its existing medical exceptions process to satisfy this requirement. A health plan company shall grant an override to the step therapy protocol if at least one of the following conditions exist:</p><p>(1) the prescription drug required under the step therapy protocol is contraindicated pursuant to the pharmaceutical manufacturer's prescribing information for the drug or, due to a documented adverse event with a previous use or a documented medical condition, including a comorbid condition, is likely to do any of the following:</p><p>(i) cause an adverse reaction to the enrollee;</p><p>(ii) decrease the ability of the enrollee to achieve or maintain reasonable functional ability in performing daily activities; or</p><p>(iii) cause physical or mental harm to the enrollee;</p><p>(2) the enrollee has had a trial of the required prescription drug covered by their current or previous health plan, or another prescription drug in the same pharmacologic class or with the same mechanism of action, and was adherent during such trial for a period of time sufficient to allow for a positive treatment outcome, and the prescription drug was discontinued by the enrollee's health care provider due to lack of effectiveness, or an adverse event. This clause does not prohibit a health plan company from requiring an enrollee to try another drug in the same pharmacologic class or with the same mechanism of action if that therapy sequence is supported by the evidence-based and peer-reviewed clinical practice guideline, Food and Drug Administration label, or pharmaceutical manufacturer's prescribing information. This clause does not apply to the commissioner of human services or a managed care plan, county-based purchasing plan, or integrated health partnership administering a pharmacy benefit under chapter 256B or 256L;</p><p>(3) for the fee-for-service system administered by the commissioner of human services, or a managed care plan, county-based purchasing plan, or integrated health partnership administering a pharmacy benefit under chapter 256B or 256L, the enrollee has had a trial of the required prescription drug covered by their current or previous health plan, or a drug in the same pharmacological class with the same mechanism of action, and was adherent during such trial for a period of time sufficient to allow for a positive treatment outcome, and the prescription drug was discontinued by the enrollee's health care provider due to lack of effectiveness, or an adverse event, or the prescriber submits an evidence-based and peer-reviewed clinical practice guideline supporting the use of the requested drug over the required prescription drug. This clause does not prohibit a managed care plan, county-based purchasing plan, or integrated health partnership from requiring an enrollee to try another drug in the same pharmacologic class with the same mechanism of action if that therapy sequence is supported by the evidence-based and peer-reviewed clinical practice guideline, Food and Drug Administration label, or pharmaceutical manufacturer's prescribing information; or</p><p>(4) the enrollee is currently receiving a positive therapeutic outcome on a prescription drug for the medical condition under consideration if, while on their current health plan or the immediately preceding health plan, the enrollee received coverage for the prescription drug and the enrollee's prescribing health care provider gives documentation to the health plan company that the change in prescription drug required by the step therapy protocol is expected to be ineffective or cause harm to the enrollee based on the known characteristics of the specific enrollee and the known characteristics of the required prescription drug.</p><p>(b) Upon granting a step therapy override, a health plan company shall authorize coverage for the prescription drug if the prescription drug is a covered prescription drug under the enrollee's health plan.</p><p>(c) The enrollee, or the prescribing health care provider if designated by the enrollee, may appeal the denial of a step therapy override by a health plan company using the complaint procedure under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.68">62Q.68</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.73">62Q.73</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.045">256.045</a>.</p><p>(d) In a denial of an override request and any subsequent appeal, a health plan company's decision must specifically state why the step therapy override request did not meet the condition under paragraph (a) cited by the prescribing health care provider in requesting the step therapy override and information regarding the procedure to request external review of the denial pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.73">62Q.73</a>. A denial of a request for a step therapy override that is upheld on appeal is a final adverse determination for purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.73">62Q.73</a> and is eligible for a request for external review by an enrollee pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.73">62Q.73</a>.</p><p>(e) A health plan company shall respond to a step therapy override request or an appeal within five days of receipt of a complete request. In cases where exigent circumstances exist, a health plan company shall respond within 72 hours of receipt of a complete request. If a health plan company does not send a response to the enrollee or prescribing health care provider if designated by the enrollee within the time allotted, the override request or appeal is granted and binding on the health plan company.</p><p>(f) Step therapy override requests must be accessible to and submitted by health care providers, and accepted by group purchasers electronically through secure electronic transmission, as described under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62J.497#stat.62J.497.5">62J.497, subdivision 5</a>.</p><p>(g) Nothing in this section prohibits a health plan company from:</p><p>(1) requesting relevant documentation from an enrollee's medical record in support of a step therapy override request; or</p><p>(2) requiring an enrollee to try a generic equivalent drug pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.21">151.21</a>, or a biosimilar, as defined under United States Code, chapter 42, section 262(i)(2), prior to providing coverage for the equivalent branded prescription drug.</p><p>(h) This section shall not be construed to allow the use of a pharmaceutical sample for the primary purpose of meeting the requirements for a step therapy override.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.184.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/162/#laws.0.1.0">2018 c 162 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.7.3.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 7 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/115/#laws.4.8.0">2020 c 115 art 4 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/58/#laws.0.11.0">2022 c 58 s 11</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.1841"><h1 class="shn">62Q.1841 PROHIBITION ON USE OF STEP THERAPY FOR METASTATIC CANCER.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.1841.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2><p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following definitions apply.</p><p>(b) "Health plan" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.01#stat.62Q.01.3">62Q.01, subdivision 3</a>. Health plan includes health coverage provided by a county-based purchasing plan participating in a public program under chapter 256B or 256L or an integrated health partnership under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0755">256B.0755</a>.</p><p>(c) "Stage four advanced metastatic cancer" means cancer that has spread from the primary or original site of the cancer to nearby tissues, lymph nodes, or other parts of the body.</p><p>(d) "Step therapy protocol" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.184#stat.62Q.184.1">62Q.184, subdivision 1</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.1841.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Prohibition on use of step therapy protocols.</span></h2><p>A health plan that provides coverage for the treatment of stage four advanced metastatic cancer or associated conditions must not limit or exclude coverage for a drug approved by the United States Food and Drug Administration that is on the health plan's prescription drug formulary by mandating that an enrollee with stage four advanced metastatic cancer or associated conditions follow a step therapy protocol if the use of the approved drug is consistent with:</p><p>(1) a United States Food and Drug Administration-approved indication; and</p><p>(2) a clinical practice guideline published by the National Comprehensive Care Network.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.1841.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.8.13.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 8 s 13</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.185"><h1 class="shn">62Q.185 GUARANTEED RENEWABILITY;
