			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/237/#laws.0.13.0">1997 c 237 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.16.8.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 16 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.113.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 113</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.585"><h1 class="shn">62Q.585 GENDER-AFFIRMING CARE COVERAGE; MEDICALLY NECESSARY CARE.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.585.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requirement.</span></h2><p>No health plan that covers physical or mental health services may be offered, sold, issued, or renewed in this state that:</p><p>(1) excludes coverage for medically necessary gender-affirming care; or</p><p>(2) requires gender-affirming treatments to satisfy a definition of "medically necessary care," "medical necessity," or any similar term that is more restrictive than the definition provided in subdivision 2.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.585.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Minimum definition.</span></h2><p>"Medically necessary care" means health care services appropriate in terms of type, frequency, level, setting, and duration to the enrollee's diagnosis or condition and diagnostic testing and preventive services. Medically necessary care must be consistent with generally accepted practice parameters as determined by health care providers in the same or similar general specialty as typically manages the condition, procedure, or treatment at issue and must:</p><p>(1) help restore or maintain the enrollee's health; or</p><p>(2) prevent deterioration of the enrollee's condition.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.585.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2><p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p><p>(b) "Gender-affirming care" means all medical, surgical, counseling, or referral services, including telehealth services, that an individual may receive to support and affirm the individual's gender identity or gender expression and that are legal under the laws of this state.</p><p>(c) "Health plan" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.01#stat.62Q.01.3">62Q.01, subdivision 3</a>, but includes the coverages listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.011#stat.62A.011.3">62A.011, subdivision 3</a>, clauses (7) and (10).</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.585.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/114/#laws.1.7.0">2024 c 114 art 1 s 7</a></p></div><div class="sr" id="stat.62Q.64"><b>62Q.64</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/247/#laws.1.32.0">2012 c 247 art 1 s 32</a>]</div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.645"><h1 class="shn">62Q.645 EFFICIENCY REPORTS AND DISTRIBUTION OF INFORMATION.</h1><p>(a) The commissioner may use reports submitted by health plan companies, service cooperatives, and the public employee insurance program created in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.316">43A.316</a> to compile entity specific administrative efficiency reports; may make these reports available on state agency websites, including minnesotahealthinfo.com; and may include information on:</p><p>(1) number of covered lives;</p><p>(2) covered services;</p><p>(3) geographic availability;</p><p>(4) whom to contact to obtain current premium rates;</p><p>(5) administrative costs, using the definition of administrative costs developed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62J.38">62J.38</a>;</p><p>(6) Internet links to information on the health plan, if available; and</p><p>(7) any other information about the health plan identified by the commissioner as being useful for employers, consumers, providers, and others in evaluating health plan options.</p><p>(b) This section does not apply to a health plan company unless its annual Minnesota premiums exceed $50,000,000 based on the most recent assessment base of the Minnesota Comprehensive Health Association. For purposes of this determination, the premiums of a health plan company include those of its affiliates.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.645.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/255/#laws.0.34.0">2006 c 255 s 34</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.65"><h1 class="shn">62Q.65 ACCESS TO PROVIDER DISCOUNTS.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.65.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requirement.</span></h2><p>A high deductible health plan must, when used in connection with a medical savings account or health savings account, provide the enrollee access to any discounted provider fees for services covered by the high deductible health plan, regardless of whether the enrollee has satisfied the deductible for the high deductible health plan.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.65.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2><p>For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given:</p><p>(1) "high deductible health plan" has the meaning given under the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, section 220(c)(2), with respect to a medical savings account; and the meaning given under Internal Revenue Code of 1986, section 223(c)(2), with respect to a health savings account;</p><p>(2) "medical savings account" has the meaning given under the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, section 220(d)(1);</p><p>(3) "discounted provider fees" means fees contained in a provider agreement entered into by the issuer of the high deductible health plan, or an affiliate of the issuer, for use in connection with the high deductible health plan; and</p><p>(4) "health savings account" has the meaning given under the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, section 223(d).</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.65.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/225/#laws.2.46.0">1997 c 225 art 2 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/132/#laws.0.17.0">2005 c 132 s 17</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.66"><h1 class="shn">62Q.66 DURABLE MEDICAL EQUIPMENT COVERAGE.</h1><p>No health plan company that covers durable medical equipment may utilize medical coverage criteria for durable medical equipment that limits coverage solely to equipment used in the home.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.66.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/334/#laws.0.1.0">1998 c 334 s 1</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.665"><h1 class="shn">62Q.665 COVERAGE FOR ORTHOTIC AND PROSTHETIC DEVICES.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.665.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2><p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p><p>(b) "Accredited facility" means any entity that is accredited to provide comprehensive orthotic or prosthetic devices or services by a Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services approved accrediting agency.</p><p>(c) "Orthosis" means:</p><p>(1) an external medical device that is:</p><p>(i) custom-fabricated or custom-fitted to a specific patient based on the patient's unique physical condition;</p><p>(ii) applied to a part of the body to correct a deformity, provide support and protection, restrict motion, improve function, or relieve symptoms of a disease, syndrome, injury, or postoperative condition; and</p><p>(iii) deemed medically necessary by a prescribing physician or licensed health care provider who has authority in Minnesota to prescribe orthotic and prosthetic devices, supplies, and services; and</p><p>(2) any provision, repair, or replacement of a device that is furnished or performed by:</p><p>(i) an accredited facility in comprehensive orthotic services; or</p><p>(ii) a health care provider licensed in Minnesota and operating within the provider's scope of practice which allows the provider to provide orthotic or prosthetic devices, supplies, or services.</p><p>(d) "Orthotics" means:</p><p>(1) the science and practice of evaluating, measuring, designing, fabricating, assembling, fitting, adjusting, or servicing and providing the initial training necessary to accomplish the fitting of an orthotic device for the support, correction, or alleviation of a neuromuscular or musculoskeletal dysfunction, disease, injury, or deformity;</p><p>(2) evaluation, treatment, and consultation related to an orthotic device;</p><p>(3) basic observation of gait and postural analysis;</p><p>(4) assessing and designing orthosis to maximize function and provide support and alignment necessary to prevent or correct a deformity or to improve the safety and efficiency of mobility and locomotion;</p><p>(5) continuing patient care to assess the effect of an orthotic device on the patient's tissues; and</p><p>(6) proper fit and function of the orthotic device by periodic evaluation.</p><p>(e) "Prosthesis" means:</p><p>(1) an external medical device that is:</p><p>(i) used to replace or restore a missing limb, appendage, or other external human body part; and</p><p>(ii) deemed medically necessary by a prescribing physician or licensed health care provider who has authority in Minnesota to prescribe orthotic and prosthetic devices, supplies, and services; and</p><p>(2) any provision, repair, or replacement of a device that is furnished or performed by:</p><p>(i) an accredited facility in comprehensive prosthetic services; or</p><p>(ii) a health care provider licensed in Minnesota and operating within the provider's scope of practice which allows the provider to provide orthotic or prosthetic devices, supplies, or services.</p><p>(f) "Prosthetics" means:</p><p>(1) the science and practice of evaluating, measuring, designing, fabricating, assembling, fitting, aligning, adjusting, or servicing, as well as providing the initial training necessary to accomplish the fitting of, a prosthesis through the replacement of external parts of a human body lost due to amputation or congenital deformities or absences;</p><p>(2) the generation of an image, form, or mold that replicates the patient's body segment and that requires rectification of dimensions, contours, and volumes for use in the design and fabrication of a socket to accept a residual anatomic limb to, in turn, create an artificial appendage that is designed either to support body weight or to improve or restore function or anatomical appearance, or both;</p><p>(3) observational gait analysis and clinical assessment of the requirements necessary to refine and mechanically fix the relative position of various parts of the prosthesis to maximize function, stability, and safety of the patient;</p><p>(4) providing and continuing patient care in order to assess the prosthetic device's effect on the patient's tissues; and</p><p>(5) assuring proper fit and function of the prosthetic device by periodic evaluation.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.665.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Coverage.</span></h2><p>(a) A health plan must provide coverage for orthotic and prosthetic devices, supplies, and services, including repair and replacement, at least equal to the coverage provided under federal law for health insurance for the aged and disabled under sections 1832, 1833, and 1834 of the Social Security Act, United States Code, title 42, sections 1395k, 1395l, and 1395m, but only to the extent consistent with this section.</p><p>(b) A health plan must not subject orthotic and prosthetic benefits to separate financial requirements that apply only with respect to those benefits. A health plan may impose co-payment and coinsurance amounts on those benefits, except that any financial requirements that apply to such benefits must not be more restrictive than the financial requirements that apply to the health plan's medical and surgical benefits, including those for internal restorative devices.</p><p>(c) A health plan may limit the benefits for, or alter the financial requirements for, out-of-network coverage of prosthetic and orthotic devices, except that the restrictions and requirements that apply to those benefits must not be more restrictive than the financial requirements that apply to the out-of-network coverage for the health plan's medical and surgical benefits.</p><p>(d) A health plan must cover orthoses and prostheses when furnished under an order by a prescribing physician or licensed health care prescriber who has authority in Minnesota to prescribe orthoses and prostheses, and that coverage for orthotic and prosthetic devices, supplies, accessories, and services must include those devices or device systems, supplies, accessories, and services that are customized to the covered individual's needs.</p><p>(e) A health plan must cover orthoses and prostheses determined by the enrollee's provider to be the most appropriate model that meets the medical needs of the enrollee for purposes of performing physical activities, as applicable, including but not limited to running, biking, and swimming, and maximizing the enrollee's limb function.</p><p>(f) A health plan must cover orthoses and prostheses for showering or bathing.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.665.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Prior authorization.</span></h2><p>A health plan may require prior authorization for orthotic and prosthetic devices, supplies, and services in the same manner and to the same extent as prior authorization is required for any other covered benefit.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.665.4"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Reimbursement.</span></h2><p>(a) The commissioner of commerce must reimburse health plan companies for coverage under this section. Reimbursement is available only for coverage that would not have been provided by the health plan without the requirements of this section. Treatments and services covered by the health plan as of January 1, 2024, are ineligible for payment under this subdivision by the commissioner of commerce.</p><p>(b) Health plan companies must report to the commissioner of commerce quantified costs attributable to the additional benefit under this section in a format developed by the commissioner. A health plan's coverage as of January 1, 2024, must be used by the health plan company as the basis for determining whether coverage would not have been provided by the health plan for purposes of this subdivision.</p><p>(c) The commissioner of commerce must evaluate submissions and make payments to health plan companies as provided in Code of Federal Regulations, title 45, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/155.170">155.170</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.665.5"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2><p>Each fiscal year, an amount necessary to make payments to health plan companies to defray the cost of providing coverage under this section is appropriated to the commissioner of commerce.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.665.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.57.41.0">2024 c 127 art 57 s 41</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.6651"><h1 class="shn">62Q.6651 MEDICAL NECESSITY AND NONDISCRIMINATION STANDARDS FOR COVERAGE OF PROSTHETICS OR ORTHOTICS.</h1><p>(a) When performing a utilization review for a request for coverage of prosthetic or orthotic benefits, a health plan company shall apply the most recent version of evidence-based treatment and fit criteria as recognized by relevant clinical specialists.</p><p>(b) A health plan company shall render utilization review determinations in a nondiscriminatory manner and shall not deny coverage for habilitative or rehabilitative benefits, including prosthetics or orthotics, solely on the basis of an enrollee's actual or perceived disability.</p><p>(c) A health plan company shall not deny a prosthetic or orthotic benefit for an individual with limb loss or absence that would otherwise be covered for a nondisabled person seeking medical or surgical intervention to restore or maintain the ability to perform the same physical activity.</p><p>(d) A health plan offered, issued, or renewed in Minnesota that offers coverage for prosthetics and custom orthotic devices shall include language describing an enrollee's rights pursuant to paragraphs (b) and (c) in its evidence of coverage and any benefit denial letters.</p><p>(e) A health plan that provides coverage for prosthetic or orthotic services shall ensure access to medically necessary clinical care and to prosthetic and custom orthotic devices and technology from not less than two distinct prosthetic and custom orthotic providers in the plan's provider network located in Minnesota. In the event that medically necessary covered orthotics and prosthetics are not available from an in-network provider, the health plan company shall provide processes to refer a member to an out-of-network provider and shall fully reimburse the out-of-network provider at a mutually agreed upon rate less member cost sharing determined on an in-network basis.</p><p>(f) If coverage for prosthetic or custom orthotic devices is provided, payment shall be made for the replacement of a prosthetic or custom orthotic device or for the replacement of any part of the devices, without regard to continuous use or useful lifetime restrictions, if an ordering health care provider determines that the provision of a replacement device, or a replacement part of a device, is necessary because:</p><p>(1) of a change in the physiological condition of the patient;</p><p>(2) of an irreparable change in the condition of the device or in a part of the device; or</p><p>(3) the condition of the device, or the part of the device, requires repairs and the cost of the repairs would be more than 60 percent of the cost of a replacement device or of the part being replaced.</p><p>(g) Confirmation from a prescribing health care provider may be required if the prosthetic or custom orthotic device or part being replaced is less than three years old.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.6651.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.57.42.0">2024 c 127 art 57 s 42</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.666"><h1 class="shn">62Q.666 INTERMITTENT CATHETERS.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.666.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Required coverage.</span></h2><p>A health plan must provide coverage for intermittent urinary catheters and insertion supplies if intermittent catheterization is recommended by the enrollee's health care provider. At least 180 intermittent catheters per month with insertion supplies must be covered unless a lesser amount is prescribed by the enrollee's health care provider. A health plan providing coverage under the medical assistance program may be required to provide coverage for more than 180 intermittent catheters per month with insertion supplies.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.666.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Cost-sharing requirements.</span></h2><p>A health plan is prohibited from imposing a deductible, co-payment, coinsurance, or other restriction on intermittent catheters and insertion supplies that the health plan does not apply to durable medical equipment in general.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.666.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.57.43.0">2024 c 127 art 57 s 43</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.67"><h1 class="shn">62Q.67 DISCLOSURE OF COVERED DURABLE MEDICAL EQUIPMENT.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.67.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Disclosure.</span></h2><p>A health plan company that covers durable medical equipment shall provide enrollees, and upon request prospective enrollees, written disclosure that includes the information set forth in subdivision 2. The health plan company may include the information in the member contract, certificate of coverage, schedule of payments, member handbook, or other written enrollee communication.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.67.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Information to be disclosed.</span></h2><p>A health plan company that covers durable medical equipment shall disclose the following information:</p><p>(1) general descriptions of the coverage for durable medical equipment, level of coverage available, and criteria and procedures for any required prior authorizations; and</p><p>(2) the address and telephone number of a health plan representative whom an enrollee may contact to obtain specific information verbally, or upon request in writing, about prior authorization including criteria used in making coverage decisions and information on limitations or exclusions for durable medical equipment.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.67.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/334/#laws.0.2.0">1998 c 334 s 2</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.675"><h1 class="shn">62Q.675 HEARING AIDS.</h1><p>A health plan must cover hearing aids for all individuals for hearing loss that is not correctable by other covered procedures. Coverage required under this section is limited to one hearing aid in each ear every three years. No special deductible, coinsurance, co-payment, or other limitation on the coverage under this section that is not generally applicable to other coverages under the plan may be imposed.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.675.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.7.24.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 7 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/60/#laws.0.1.0">2007 c 60 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.3.2.0">2023 c 70 art 3 s 2</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.676"><h1 class="shn">62Q.676 MEDICATION THERAPY MANAGEMENT.</h1><p class="in">A pharmacy benefit manager that provides prescription drug services must make available medication therapy management services for enrollees taking four or more prescriptions to treat or prevent two or more chronic medical conditions. For purposes of this section, "medication therapy management" means the provision of the following pharmaceutical care services by, or under the supervision of, a licensed pharmacist to optimize the therapeutic outcomes of the patient's medications:</p><p class="in">(1) performing a comprehensive medication review to identify, resolve, and prevent medication-related problems, including adverse drug events;</p><p class="in">(2) communicating essential information to the patient's other primary care providers; and</p><p class="in">(3) providing verbal education and training designed to enhance patient understanding and appropriate use of the patient's medications.</p><p class="in">Nothing in this section shall be construed to expand or modify the scope of practice of the pharmacist as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/151.01#stat.151.01.27">151.01, subdivision 27</a>.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.676.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/79/#laws.4.7.0">2009 c 79 art 4 s 7</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.677"><h1 class="shn">62Q.677 LIFETIME AND ANNUAL LIMITS.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.677.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability and scope.</span></h2><p>Except as provided in subdivision 2, this section applies to a health plan company providing coverage under an individual or group health plan. For purposes of this section, essential health benefits is defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.81">62Q.81</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.677.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grandfathered plan limits.</span></h2><p>(a) The prohibition on lifetime limits applies to grandfathered plans providing individual health plan coverage or group health plan coverage.</p><p>(b) The prohibition and limits on annual limits apply to grandfathered plans providing group health plan coverage, but do not apply to grandfathered plans providing individual health plan coverage.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.677.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Prohibition on lifetime and annual limits.</span></h2><p>(a) Except as provided in subdivisions 4 and 5, a health plan company offering coverage under an individual or group health plan shall not establish a lifetime limit on the dollar amount of essential health benefits for any individual.</p><p>(b) Except as provided in subdivisions 4, 5, and 6, a health plan company shall not establish any annual limit on the dollar amount of essential health benefits for any individual.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.677.4"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Nonessential benefits; out-of-network providers.</span></h2><p>(a) Subdivision 3 does not prevent a health plan company from placing annual or lifetime dollar limits for any individual on specific covered benefits that are not essential health benefits as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.81#stat.62Q.81.4">62Q.81, subdivision 4</a>, to the extent that the limits are otherwise permitted under applicable federal or state law.</p><p>(b) Subdivision 3 does not prevent a health plan company from placing an annual or lifetime limit for services provided by out-of-network providers.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.677.5"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Excluded benefits.</span></h2><p>This section does not prohibit a health plan company from excluding all benefits for a given condition.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.677.6"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Annual limits prior to January 1, 2014.</span></h2><p>For plan or policy years beginning before January 1, 2014, for any individual, a health plan company may establish an annual limit on the dollar amount of benefits that are essential health benefits provided the limit is no less than the following:</p><p>(1) for a plan or policy year beginning after September 22, 2010, but before September 23, 2011, $750,000;</p><p>(2) for a plan or policy year beginning after September 22, 2011, but before September 23, 2012, $1,250,000; and</p><p>(3) for a plan or policy year beginning after September 22, 2012, but before January 1, 2014, $2,000,000.</p><p>In determining whether an individual has received benefits that meet or exceed the allowable limits, a health plan company shall take into account only essential health benefits.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.677.7"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Waivers.</span></h2><p>For plan or policy years beginning before January 1, 2014, a health plan is exempt from the annual limit requirements if the health plan is approved for a waiver from the requirements by the United States Department of Health and Human Services, but the exemption only applies for the specified period of time that the waiver from the United States Department of Health and Human Services is applicable.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.677.8"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Notices.</span></h2><p>(a) At the time a health plan company receives a waiver from the United States Department of Health and Human Services, the health plan company shall notify prospective applicants and affected policyholders and the commissioner in each state where prospective applicants and any affected insured are known to reside.</p><p>(b) At the time the waiver expires or is otherwise no longer in effect, the health plan company shall notify affected policyholders and the commissioner in each state where any affected insured is known to reside.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.677.9"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Reinstatement.</span></h2><p>A health plan company shall comply with all provisions of the Affordable Care Act with regard to reinstatement of coverage for individuals whose coverage or benefits under a health plan ended by reason of reaching a lifetime dollar limit on the dollar value of all benefits for the individual.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.677.10"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Compliance.</span></h2><p>This section does not require compliance with any provision of the Affordable Care Act before the effective date provided for that provision in the Affordable Care Act. The commissioner shall enforce this section.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.677.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/84/#laws.1.80.0">2013 c 84 art 1 s 80</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.678"><h1 class="shn">62Q.678 DEPENDENT CHILD NOTICE.</h1><p>Group health plans and health plan companies that offer group or individual health plans with dependent coverage must provide written notice to an enrollee with dependent child coverage that the dependent child's coverage ends when the child reaches the age of 26. Notice must be sent to the enrollee at the enrollee's last known address at least 60 days before the dependent child reaches the age of 26. The notice must include the date on which coverage ends and information on accessing the MNsure website as applicable.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.678.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/73/#laws.0.2.0">2020 c 73 s 2</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.679"><h1 class="shn">62Q.679 RELIGIOUS OBJECTIONS.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.679.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2><p>(a) The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p><p>(b) "Closely held for-profit entity" means an entity that is not a nonprofit entity, has more than 50 percent of the value of its ownership interest owned directly or indirectly by five or fewer owners, and has no publicly traded ownership interest. For purposes of this paragraph:</p><p>(1) ownership interests owned by a corporation, partnership, limited liability company, estate, trust, or similar entity are considered owned by that entity's shareholders, partners, members, or beneficiaries in proportion to their interest held in the corporation, partnership, limited liability company, estate, trust, or similar entity;</p><p>(2) ownership interests owned by a nonprofit entity are considered owned by a single owner;</p><p>(3) ownership interests owned by all individuals in a family are considered held by a single owner. For purposes of this clause, "family" means brothers and sisters, including half-brothers and half-sisters, a spouse, ancestors, and lineal descendants; and</p><p>(4) if an individual or entity holds an option, warrant, or similar right to purchase an ownership interest, the individual or entity is considered to be the owner of those ownership interests.</p><p>(c) "Eligible organization" means an organization that opposes covering some or all health benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.524">62Q.524</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a> on account of religious objections and that is:</p><p>(1) organized as a nonprofit entity and holds itself out to be religious; or</p><p>(2) organized and operates as a closely held for-profit entity, and the organization's owners or highest governing body has adopted, under the organization's applicable rules of governance and consistent with state law, a resolution or similar action establishing that the organization objects to covering some or all health benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.524">62Q.524</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a> on account of the owners' sincerely held religious beliefs.</p><p>(d) "Exempt organization" means an organization that is organized and operates as a nonprofit entity and meets the requirements of section 6033(a)(3)(A)(i) or (iii) of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, as amended.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.679.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exemption.</span></h2><p>(a) An exempt organization is not required to provide coverage under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.524">62Q.524</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a> if the exempt organization has religious objections to the coverage. An exempt organization that chooses to not provide coverage pursuant to this paragraph must notify employees as part of the hiring process and must notify all employees at least 30 days before:</p><p>(1) an employee enrolls in the health plan; or</p><p>(2) the effective date of the health plan, whichever occurs first.</p><p>(b) If the exempt organization provides partial coverage under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.524">62Q.524</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a>, the notice required under paragraph (a) must provide a list of the portions of such coverage which the organization refuses to cover.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.679.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Accommodation for eligible organizations.</span></h2><p>(a) A health plan established or maintained by an eligible organization complies with the coverage requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.524">62Q.524</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a>, with respect to the health benefits identified in the notice under this paragraph, if the eligible organization provides notice to any health plan company with which the eligible organization contracts that it is an eligible organization and that the eligible organization has a religious objection to coverage for all or a subset of the health benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.524">62Q.524</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a>.</p><p>(b) The notice from an eligible organization to a health plan company under paragraph (a) must include: (1) the name of the eligible organization; (2) a statement that it objects to coverage for some or all of the health benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.524">62Q.524</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a>, including a list of the health benefits to which the eligible organization objects, if applicable; and (3) the health plan name. The notice must be executed by a person authorized to provide notice on behalf of the eligible organization.</p><p>(c) An eligible organization must provide a copy of the notice under paragraph (a) to prospective employees as part of the hiring process and to all employees at least 30 days before:</p><p>(1) an employee enrolls in the health plan; or</p><p>(2) the effective date of the health plan, whichever occurs first.</p><p>(d) A health plan company that receives a copy of the notice under paragraph (a) with respect to a health plan established or maintained by an eligible organization must, for all future enrollments in the health plan:</p><p>(1) expressly exclude coverage for those health benefits identified in the notice under paragraph (a) from the health plan; and</p><p>(2) provide separate payments for any health benefits required to be covered under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.524">62Q.524</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a> for enrollees as long as the enrollee remains enrolled in the health plan.</p><p>(e) The health plan company must not impose any cost-sharing requirements, including co-pays, deductibles, or coinsurance, or directly or indirectly impose any premium, fee, or other charge for the health benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a> on the enrollee. The health plan company must not directly or indirectly impose any premium, fee, or other charge for the health benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.524">62Q.524</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a> on the eligible organization or health plan.</p><p>(f) On January 1, 2025, and every year thereafter a health plan company must notify the commissioner, in a manner determined by the commissioner, of the number of eligible organizations granted an accommodation under this subdivision.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.679.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/114/#laws.1.8.0">2024 c 114 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.57.44.0">2024 c 127 art 57 s 44</a>,69</p></div><p><b>NOTE: </b>This section, as added by Laws 2024, chapter 127, article 57, section 44, supersedes the section as added in Senate File 4097, article 1, section 8. Laws 2024, chapter 127, article 57, section 69.</p><p><b>NOTE: </b>This section was also added by Laws 2024, chapter 114, article 1, section 8, to read as follows:</p>"62Q.679 RELIGIOUS OBJECTIONS.<div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.679.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2><p>(a) The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p><p>(b) "Closely held for-profit entity" means an entity that is not a nonprofit entity, has more than 50 percent of the value of its ownership interest owned directly or indirectly by five or fewer owners, and has no publicly traded ownership interest. For purposes of this paragraph:</p><p>(1) ownership interests owned by a corporation, partnership, limited liability company, estate, trust, or similar entity are considered owned by that entity's shareholders, partners, members, or beneficiaries in proportion to their interest held in the corporation, partnership, limited liability company, estate, trust, or similar entity;</p><p>(2) ownership interests owned by a nonprofit entity are considered owned by a single owner;</p><p>(3) ownership interests owned by all individuals in a family are considered held by a single owner. For purposes of this clause, "family" means brothers and sisters, including half-brothers and half-sisters, a spouse, ancestors, and lineal descendants; and</p><p>(4) if an individual or entity holds an option, warrant, or similar right to purchase an ownership interest, the individual or entity is considered to be the owner of those ownership interests.</p><p>(c) "Eligible organization" means an organization that opposes covering some or all health benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a> on account of religious objections and that is:</p><p>(1) organized as a nonprofit entity and holds itself out to be religious; or</p><p>(2) organized and operates as a closely held for-profit entity, and the organization's owners or highest governing body has adopted, under the organization's applicable rules of governance and consistent with state law, a resolution or similar action establishing that the organization objects to covering some or all health benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a> on account of the owners' sincerely held religious beliefs.</p><p>(d) "Exempt organization" means an organization that is organized and operates as a nonprofit entity and meets the requirements of section 6033(a)(3)(A)(i) or (iii) of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, as amended.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.679.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exemption.</span></h2><p>(a) An exempt organization is not required to provide coverage under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a> if the exempt organization has religious objections to the coverage. An exempt organization that chooses to not provide coverage pursuant to this paragraph must notify employees as part of the hiring process and must notify all employees at least 30 days before:</p><p>(1) an employee enrolls in the health plan; or</p><p>(2) the effective date of the health plan, whichever occurs first.</p><p>(b) If the exempt organization provides partial coverage under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a>, the notice required under paragraph (a) must provide a list of the portions of such coverage which the organization refuses to cover.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.679.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Accommodation for eligible organizations.</span></h2><p>(a) A health plan established or maintained by an eligible organization complies with the coverage requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a>, with respect to the health benefits identified in the notice under this paragraph, if the eligible organization provides notice to any health plan company with which the eligible organization contracts that it is an eligible organization and that the eligible organization has a religious objection to coverage for all or a subset of the health benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a>.</p><p>(b) The notice from an eligible organization to a health plan company under paragraph (a) must include: (1) the name of the eligible organization; (2) a statement that it objects to coverage for some or all of the health benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a>, including a list of the health benefits to which the eligible organization objects, if applicable; and (3) the health plan name. The notice must be executed by a person authorized to provide notice on behalf of the eligible organization.</p><p>(c) An eligible organization must provide a copy of the notice under paragraph (a) to prospective employees as part of the hiring process and to all employees at least 30 days before:</p><p>(1) an employee enrolls in the health plan; or</p><p>(2) the effective date of the health plan, whichever occurs first.</p><p>(d) A health plan company that receives a copy of the notice under paragraph (a) with respect to a health plan established or maintained by an eligible organization must, for all future enrollments in the health plan:</p><p>(1) expressly exclude coverage for those health benefits identified in the notice under paragraph (a) from the health plan; and</p><p>(2) provide separate payments for any health benefits required to be covered under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a> for enrollees as long as the enrollee remains enrolled in the health plan.</p><p>(e) The health plan company must not impose any cost-sharing requirements, including co-pays, deductibles, or coinsurance, or directly or indirectly impose any premium, fee, or other charge for the health benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a> on the enrollee. The health plan company must not directly or indirectly impose any premium, fee, or other charge for the health benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.522">62Q.522</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.585">62Q.585</a> on the eligible organization or health plan.</p><p>(f) On January 1, 2024, and every year thereafter a health plan company must notify the commissioner, in a manner determined by the commissioner, of the number of eligible organizations granted an accommodation under this subdivision."</p></div><h1 class="fgh">COMPLAINT RESOLUTION</h1><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.68"><h1 class="shn">62Q.68 DEFINITIONS.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.68.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2><p>For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.68">62Q.68</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.72">62Q.72</a>, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given them. For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.69">62Q.69 </a>and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.70">62Q.70</a>, the term "health plan company" does not include an insurance company licensed under chapter 60A to offer, sell, or issue a policy of accident and sickness insurance as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62A.01">62A.01</a> or a nonprofit health service plan corporation regulated under chapter 62C that only provides dental coverage or vision coverage. For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.69">62Q.69</a> through <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.73">62Q.73</a>, the term "health plan company" does not include the Comprehensive Health Association created under chapter 62E. Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.70">62Q.70</a> does not apply to individual coverage. However, a health plan company offering individual coverage may, pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.69#stat.62Q.69.3">62Q.69, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (c), follow the process outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.70">62Q.70</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.68.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Complaint.</span></h2><p>"Complaint" means any grievance against a health plan company that is not the subject of litigation and that has been submitted by a complainant to a health plan company regarding the provision of health services including, but not limited to, the scope of coverage for health care services; retrospective denials or limitations of payment for services; eligibility issues; denials, cancellations, or nonrenewals of coverage; administrative operations; and the quality, timeliness, and appropriateness of health care services rendered. If the complaint is from an applicant, the complaint must relate to the application. If the complaint is from a former enrollee, the complaint must relate to services received during the period of time the individual was an enrollee. Any grievance requiring a medical determination in its resolution must have the medical determination aspect of the complaint processed under the appeal procedure described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62M.06">62M.06</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.68.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Complainant.</span></h2><p>"Complainant" means an enrollee, applicant, or former enrollee, or anyone acting on behalf of an enrollee, applicant, or former enrollee, who submits a complaint.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.68.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/239/#laws.0.34.0">1999 c 239 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/330/#laws.0.28.0">2002 c 330 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/84/#laws.1.81.0">2013 c 84 art 1 s 81</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.69"><h1 class="shn">62Q.69 COMPLAINT RESOLUTION.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.69.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2><p>Each health plan company must establish and maintain an internal complaint resolution process that meets the requirements of this section to provide for the resolution of a complaint initiated by a complainant.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.69.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Procedures for filing a complaint.</span></h2><p>(a) A complainant may submit a complaint to a health plan company either by telephone or in writing. If a complaint is submitted orally and the resolution of the complaint, as determined by the complainant, is partially or wholly adverse to the complainant, or the oral complaint is not resolved to the satisfaction of the complainant, by the health plan company within ten days of receiving the complaint, the health plan company must inform the complainant that the complaint may be submitted in writing. The health plan company must also offer to provide the complainant with any assistance needed to submit a written complaint, including an offer to complete the complaint form for a complaint that was previously submitted orally and promptly mail the completed form to the complainant for the complainant's signature. At the complainant's request, the health plan company must provide the assistance requested by the complainant. The complaint form must include the following information:</p><p>(1) the telephone number of the health plan company member services or other departments or persons equipped to advise complainants on complaint resolution;</p><p>(2) the address to which the form must be sent;</p><p>(3) a description of the health plan company's internal complaint procedure and the applicable time limits; and</p><p>(4) the toll-free telephone number of either the commissioner of health or commerce and notification that the complainant has the right to submit the complaint at any time to the appropriate commissioner for investigation.</p><p>(b) Upon receipt of a written complaint, the health plan company must notify the complainant within ten business days that the complaint was received, unless the complaint is resolved to the satisfaction of the complainant within the ten business days.</p><p>(c) Each health plan company must provide, in the member handbook, subscriber contract, or certification of coverage, a clear and concise description of how to submit a complaint and a statement that, upon request, assistance in submitting a written complaint is available from the health plan company.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.69.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notification of complaint decisions.</span></h2><p>(a) The health plan company must notify the complainant in writing of its decision and the reasons for it as soon as practical but in no case later than 30 days after receipt of a written complaint. If the health plan company cannot make a decision within 30 days due to circumstances outside the control of the health plan company, the health plan company may take up to 14 additional days to notify the complainant of its decision. If the health plan company takes any additional days beyond the initial 30-day period to make its decision, it must inform the complainant, in advance, of the extension and the reasons for the extension.</p><p>(b) For group health plans, if the decision is partially or wholly adverse to the complainant, the notification must inform the complainant of the right to appeal the decision to the health plan company's internal appeal process described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.70">62Q.70 </a>and the procedure for initiating an appeal.</p><p>(c) For individual health plans, if the decision is partially or wholly adverse to the complainant, the notification must inform the complainant of the right to submit the complaint decision to the external review process described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.73">62Q.73</a> and the procedure for initiating the external review process. Notwithstanding the provisions in this subdivision, a health plan company offering individual coverage may instead follow the process for group health plans outlined in paragraph (b).</p><p>(d) The notification must also inform the complainant of the right to submit the complaint at any time to either the commissioner of health or commerce for investigation and the toll-free telephone number of the appropriate commissioner.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.69.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/239/#laws.0.35.0">1999 c 239 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/221/#laws.0.1.0">2008 c 221 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/84/#laws.1.82.0">2013 c 84 art 1 s 82</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.70"><h1 class="shn">62Q.70 APPEAL OF THE COMPLAINT DECISION.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.70.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2><p>(a) Each health plan company shall establish an internal appeal process for reviewing a health plan company's decision regarding a complaint filed in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.69">62Q.69</a>. The appeal process must meet the requirements of this section. This section applies only to group health plans. However, a health plan company offering individual coverage may, pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.69">62Q.69</a>, subdivision 3, paragraph (c), follow the process outlined in this section.</p><p>(b) The person or persons with authority to resolve or recommend the resolution of the internal appeal must not be solely the same person or persons who made the complaint decision under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.69">62Q.69</a>.</p><p>(c) The internal appeal process must permit the enrollee to review the information relied upon in the course of the appeal and the receipt of testimony, correspondence, explanations, or other information from the complainant, staff persons, administrators, providers, or other persons as deemed necessary by the person or persons investigating or presiding over the appeal.</p><p>(d) The enrollee must be allowed to receive continued coverage pending the outcome of the appeals process.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.70.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Procedures for filing an appeal.</span></h2><p>The health plan company must provide notice to enrollees of its internal appeals process in a culturally and linguistically appropriate manner consistent with the provisions of the Affordable Care Act. If a complainant notifies the health plan company of the complainant's desire to appeal the health plan company's decision regarding the complaint through the internal appeal process, the health plan company must provide the complainant the option for the appeal to occur either in writing or by hearing.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.70.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notification of appeal decisions.</span></h2><p>(a) If a complainant appeals in writing, the health plan company must give the complainant written notice of the appeal decision and all key findings within 30 days of the health plan company's receipt of the complainant's written notice of appeal. If a complainant appeals by hearing, the health plan company must give the complainant written notice of the appeal decision and all key findings within 45 days of the health plan company's receipt of the complainant's written notice of appeal.</p><p>(b) If the appeal decision is partially or wholly adverse to the complainant, the notice must advise the complainant of the right to submit the appeal decision to the external review process described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.73">62Q.73</a> and the procedure for initiating the external process.</p><p>(c) Upon the request of the complainant, the health plan company must provide the complainant with a complete summary of the appeal decision.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.70.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/239/#laws.0.36.0">1999 c 239 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/84/#laws.1.83.0">2013 c 84 art 1 s 83</a>,84</p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.71"><h1 class="shn">62Q.71 NOTICE TO ENROLLEES.</h1><p>Each health plan company shall provide to enrollees a clear and concise description of its complaint resolution procedure, if applicable under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.68#stat.62Q.68.1">62Q.68, subdivision 1</a>, and the procedure used for utilization review as defined under chapter 62M as part of the member handbook, subscriber contract, or certificate of coverage. If the health plan company does not issue a member handbook, the health plan company may provide the description in another written document. The description must specifically inform enrollees:</p><p>(1) how to submit a complaint to the health plan company;</p><p>(2) if the health plan includes utilization review requirements, how to notify the utilization review organization in a timely manner and how to obtain authorization for health care services;</p><p>(3) how to request an appeal either through the procedures described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.70">62Q.70</a>, if applicable, or through the procedures described in chapter 62M;</p><p>(4) of the right to file a complaint with either the commissioner of health or commerce at any time during the complaint and appeal process;</p><p>(5) of the toll-free telephone number of the appropriate commissioner; and</p><p>(6) of the right, for individual and group coverage, to obtain an external review under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.73">62Q.73</a> and a description of when and how that right may be exercised, including that under most circumstances an enrollee must exhaust the internal complaint or appeal process prior to external review. However, an enrollee may proceed to external review without exhausting the internal complaint or appeal process under the following circumstances:</p><p>(i) the health plan company waives the exhaustion requirement;</p><p>(ii) the health plan company is considered to have waived the exhaustion requirement by failing to substantially comply with any requirements including, but not limited to, time limits for internal complaints or appeals; or</p><p>(iii) the enrollee has applied for an expedited external review at the same time the enrollee has applied for internal review under chapter 62M.</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.71.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/239/#laws.0.37.0">1999 c 239 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/2/#laws.1.8.0">2003 c 2 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/84/#laws.1.85.0">2013 c 84 art 1 s 85</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/114/#laws.2.17.0">2020 c 114 art 2 s 17</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.72"><h1 class="shn">62Q.72 RECORD KEEPING; REPORTING.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.72.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Record keeping.</span></h2><p>Each health plan company shall maintain records of all enrollee complaints and their resolutions. These records shall be retained for five years and shall be made available to the appropriate commissioner upon request. An insurance company licensed under chapter 60A may instead comply with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/72A.20#stat.72A.20.30">72A.20, subdivision 30</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.72.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2><p>Each health plan company shall submit to the appropriate commissioner, as part of the company's annual filing, data on the number and type of complaints that are not resolved within 30 days, or 30 business days as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/72A.201#stat.72A.201.4">72A.201, subdivision 4</a>, clause (3), for insurance companies licensed under chapter 60A. The commissioner shall also make this information available to the public upon request.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.72.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/239/#laws.0.38.0">1999 c 239 s 38</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.73"><h1 class="shn">62Q.73 EXTERNAL REVIEW OF ADVERSE DETERMINATIONS.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.73.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2><p>For purposes of this section, "adverse determination" means:</p><p>(1) for individual health plans, a complaint decision relating to a health care service or claim that is partially or wholly adverse to the complainant;</p><p>(2) an individual health plan that is grandfathered plan coverage may instead apply the definition of adverse determination for group coverage in clause (3);</p><p>(3) for group health plans, a complaint decision relating to a health care service or claim that has been appealed in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.70">62Q.70</a> and the appeal decision is partially or wholly adverse to the complainant;</p><p>(4) any adverse determination, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62M.02">62M.02</a>, subdivision 1a, that has been appealed in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62M.06">62M.06</a> and the appeal did not reverse the adverse determination;</p><p>(5) a decision relating to a health care service made by a health plan company licensed under chapter 60A that denies the service on the basis that the service was not medically necessary;</p><p>(6) the enrollee has met the requirements of subdivision 6, paragraph (e); or</p><p>(7) a decision relating to a health plan's coverage of nonparticipating provider services as described in and subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.556#stat.62Q.556.1">62Q.556, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a).</p><p class="bl">An adverse determination does not include complaints relating to fraudulent marketing practices or agent misrepresentation.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.73.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exception.</span></h2><p>(a) This section does not apply to governmental programs except as permitted under paragraph (b). For purposes of this subdivision, "governmental programs" means the prepaid medical assistance program; effective January 1, 2026, the medical assistance fee-for-service program; the MinnesotaCare program; the demonstration project for people with disabilities; and the federal Medicare program.</p><p>(b) In the course of a recipient's appeal of a medical determination to the commissioner of human services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.045">256.045</a>, the recipient may request an expert medical opinion be arranged by the external review entity under contract to provide independent external reviews under this section. If such a request is made, the cost of the review shall be paid by the commissioner of human services. Any medical opinion obtained under this paragraph shall only be used by a state human services judge as evidence in the recipient's appeal to the commissioner of human services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.045">256.045</a>.</p><p>(c) Nothing in this subdivision shall be construed to limit or restrict the appeal rights provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.045">256.045</a> for governmental program recipients.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.73.3"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Right to external review.</span></h2><p>(a) Any enrollee or anyone acting on behalf of an enrollee who has received an adverse determination may submit a written request for an external review of the adverse determination, if applicable under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.68#stat.62Q.68.1">62Q.68, subdivision 1</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62M.06">62M.06</a>, to the commissioner of health if the request involves a health plan company regulated by that commissioner or to the commissioner of commerce if the request involves a health plan company regulated by that commissioner. Notification of the enrollee's right to external review must accompany the denial issued by the insurer.</p><p>(b) Nothing in this section requires the commissioner of health or commerce to independently investigate an adverse determination referred for independent external review.</p><p>(c) If an enrollee requests an external review, the health plan company must participate in the external review. The cost of the external review must be borne by the health plan company.</p><p>(d) The enrollee must request external review within six months from the date of the adverse determination.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.73.4"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Contract.</span></h2><p>Pursuant to a request for proposal, the commissioner of administration, in consultation with the commissioners of health and commerce, shall contract with at least three organizations or business entities to provide independent external reviews of all adverse determinations submitted for external review. The contract shall ensure that the fees for services rendered in connection with the reviews are reasonable.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.73.5"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Criteria.</span></h2><p> The request for proposal must require that the entity demonstrate:</p><p>(1) no conflicts of interest in that it is not owned, a subsidiary of, or affiliated with a health plan company, utilization review organization, or a trade organization of health care providers;</p><p>(2) an expertise in dispute resolution;</p><p>(3) an expertise in health-related law;</p><p>(4) an ability to conduct reviews using a variety of alternative dispute resolution procedures depending upon the nature of the dispute;</p><p>(5) an ability to maintain written records, for at least three years, regarding reviews conducted and provide data to the commissioners of health and commerce upon request on reviews conducted;</p><p>(6) an ability to ensure confidentiality of medical records and other enrollee information;</p><p>(7) accreditation by nationally recognized private accrediting organization; and</p><p>(8) the ability to provide an expedited external review process.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.73.6"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Process.</span></h2><p>(a) Upon receiving a request for an external review, the commissioner shall assign an external review entity on a random basis. The assigned external review entity must provide immediate notice of the review to the enrollee and to the health plan company. Within ten business days of receiving notice of the review, the health plan company and the enrollee must provide the assigned external review entity with any information that they wish to be considered. Each party shall be provided an opportunity to present its version of the facts and arguments. The assigned external review entity must furnish to the health plan company any additional information submitted by the enrollee within one business day of receipt. An enrollee may be assisted or represented by a person of the enrollee's choice.</p><p>(b) As part of the external review process, any aspect of an external review involving a medical determination must be performed by a health care professional with expertise in the medical issue being reviewed.</p><p>(c) An external review shall be made as soon as practical but in no case later than 45 days after receiving the request for an external review and must promptly send written notice of the decision and the reasons for it to the enrollee, the health plan company, and the commissioner who is responsible for regulating the health plan company.</p><p>(d) The external review entity and the clinical reviewer assigned must not have a material professional, familial, or financial conflict of interest with:</p><p>(1) the health plan company that is the subject of the external review;</p><p>(2) the enrollee, or any parties related to the enrollee, whose treatment is the subject of the external review;</p><p>(3) any officer, director, or management employee of the health plan company;</p><p>(4) a plan administrator, plan fiduciaries, or plan employees;</p><p>(5) the health care provider, the health care provider's group, or practice association recommending treatment that is the subject of the external review;</p><p>(6) the facility at which the recommended treatment would be provided; or</p><p>(7) the developer or manufacturer of the principal drug, device, procedure, or other therapy being recommended.</p><p>(e)(1) An expedited external review must be provided if the enrollee requests it after receiving:</p><p>(i) an adverse determination that involves a medical condition for which the time frame for completion of an expedited internal appeal would seriously jeopardize the life or health of the enrollee or would jeopardize the enrollee's ability to regain maximum function and the enrollee has simultaneously requested an expedited internal appeal;</p><p>(ii) an adverse determination that concerns an admission, availability of care, continued stay, or health care service for which the enrollee received emergency services but has not been discharged from a facility; or</p><p>(iii) an adverse determination that involves a medical condition for which the standard external review time would seriously jeopardize the life or health of the enrollee or jeopardize the enrollee's ability to regain maximum function.</p><p>(2) The external review entity must make its expedited determination to uphold or reverse the adverse determination as expeditiously as possible but within no more than 72 hours after the receipt of the request for expedited review and notify the enrollee and the health plan company of the determination.</p><p>(3) If the external review entity's notification is not in writing, the external review entity must provide written confirmation of the determination within 48 hours of the notification.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.73.7"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Standards of review.</span></h2><p>(a) For an external review of any issue in an adverse determination that does not require a medical necessity determination, the external review must be based on whether the adverse determination was in compliance with the enrollee's health benefit plan or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.556#stat.62Q.556.1">62Q.556, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a).</p><p>(b) For an external review of any issue in an adverse determination by a health plan company licensed under chapter 62D that requires a medical necessity determination, the external review must determine whether the adverse determination was consistent with the definition of medically necessary care in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/4685.0100">4685.0100</a>, subpart 9b.</p><p>(c) For an external review of any issue in an adverse determination by a health plan company, other than a health plan company licensed under chapter 62D, that requires a medical necessity determination, the external review must determine whether the adverse determination was consistent with the definition of medically necessary care in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.53#stat.62Q.53.2">62Q.53, subdivision 2</a>.</p><p>(d) For an external review of an adverse determination involving experimental or investigational treatment, the external review entity must base its decision on all documents submitted by the health plan company and enrollee, including:</p><p>(1) medical records;</p><p>(2) the recommendation of the attending physician, advanced practice registered nurse, physician assistant, or health care professional;</p><p>(3) consulting reports from health care professionals;</p><p>(4) the terms of coverage;</p><p>(5) federal Food and Drug Administration approval; and</p><p>(6) medical or scientific evidence or evidence-based standards.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.73.8"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Effects of external review.</span></h2><p>A decision rendered under this section shall be nonbinding on the enrollee and binding on the health plan company. The health plan company may seek judicial review of the decision on the grounds that the decision was arbitrary and capricious or involved an abuse of discretion.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.73.9"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Immunity from civil liability.</span></h2><p>A person who participates in an external review by investigating, reviewing materials, providing technical expertise, or rendering a decision shall not be civilly liable for any action that is taken in good faith, that is within the scope of the person's duties, and that does not constitute willful or reckless misconduct.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.73.10"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Data reporting.</span></h2><p>The commissioners shall make available to the public, upon request, summary data on the decisions rendered under this section, including the number of reviews heard and decided and the final outcomes. Any data released to the public must not individually identify the enrollee initiating the request for external review.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.73.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/239/#laws.0.39.0">1999 c 239 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/474/#laws.0.2.0">2000 c 474 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/215/#laws.0.27.0">2001 c 215 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/84/#laws.1.86.0">2013 c 84 art 1 s 86</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/107/#laws.1.9.0">2013 c 107 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.2.28.0">2016 c 158 art 2 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/114/#laws.2.18.0">2020 c 114 art 2 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/115/#laws.4.13.0">2020 c 115 art 4 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/58/#laws.0.13.0">2022 c 58 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.2.32.0">2023 c 70 art 2 s 32</a>,33; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/121/#laws.4.4.0">2024 c 121 art 4 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.57.45.0">2024 c 127 art 57 s 45</a></p></div><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.731"><h1 class="shn">62Q.731 APPEAL FROM ADVERSE DETERMINATION.</h1><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.731.1"><h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2><p>(a) For purposes of this section, the terms defined in this subdivision have the meanings given.</p><p>(b) "Enrollee" means an eligible person as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62E.02#stat.62E.02.13">62E.02, subdivision 13</a>, and who meets the eligibility criteria established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62E.14">62E.14</a>.</p><p>(c) "Board" means the board of directors of the Comprehensive Health Association, as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62E.10#stat.62E.10.2">62E.10, subdivision 2</a>.</p></div><div class="subd" id="stat.62Q.731.2"><h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Appeal to external review entity.</span></h2><p>If an enrollee receives an adverse determination as a result of the Comprehensive Health Association's internal appeal process, by which an established enrollee appeal committee renders an adverse determination, the enrollee then has the option of:</p><p>(1) appealing the adverse determination to the external review entity under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.73">62Q.73</a>, which shall constitute a final determination subject to the conditions specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.73">62Q.73</a>; or</p><p>(2) appealing to the commissioner of commerce from an adverse determination as provided by the operating rules of the Comprehensive Health Association, in which case the commissioner has the option of making a determination regarding the appeal, or submitting the appeal to the external review entity retained under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.73">62Q.73</a>.</p></div></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.731.history
			"><h2>History: </h2><p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/330/#laws.0.29.0">2002 c 330 s 29</a></p></div><h1 class="fgh">MINNESOTA HEALTH PLAN CONTRACTING ACT</h1><div class="section" id="stat.62Q.732"><h1 class="shn">62Q.732 CITATION.</h1><p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.732">62Q.732</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/62Q.75">62Q.75</a> may be cited as the "Minnesota Health Plan Contracting Act."</p></div><div class="history" id="stat.62Q.732.history
